The offline Bash suite now has **76 passing controls, zero failed**, including guarded remove/revoke with running and stopped interfaces, blank/malformed/padding-invalid keys, different keys, remove/add name reuse and a waiting guarded writer whose selected key changes under the real interface lock. A two-peer regression first reproduced an incorrect successful removal: assignment whitespace made the old extractor read the following peer's key. Bounded, whitespace-tolerant extraction now rejects that wrong identity and removes only the selected peer for its matching key, for both commands. Existing explicit name-only remove/revoke schema controls retain supported operator CLI behavior. `bash -n` also passed for the affected scripts and fixture. All **18 injected Python harness controls** pass with mandatory guarded requests and exact mutation receipt keys; no SSH or disposable-host invocation occurred. Current-task and failed regression logs remain under the identity-guard worktree's ignored `.artifacts/codex-command-logs`.

These fixtures establish source/offline behavior. They do not prove Android transport/UI confirmation, cancellation/lost-response reconciliation, actual VPN access revocation, remote archive durability or live-server behavior. APK/device/live operations need separate authorization. A different or missing success receipt remains an uncertain remote outcome, never a retry target selected by name alone.

## Next Implementation Roadmap

This roadmap covers the whole app, not only the VPS fleet. Do not move items from this section into active coverage until the tests exist and run in the expected test layer.

### Phase 1 — Stabilize Current Baseline

- Keep each runtime fix with its focused regression controls and affected documentation. Separate unrelated QA framework or broad coverage expansion into another branch or commit.
- Run `:ui:testDebugUnitTest` after test or runtime changes that affect app logic.
- Run full `:ui:assembleDebug` only when explicitly requested, per project build policy.
- Treat remaining untested behavior as residual regression risk; the current suite reduces risk but does not prove no regressions exist.

### Phase 2 — Complete Missing Automated Coverage

- Retain real-library deadline/cancellation controls below; extend them when supported auth, crypto or channel behavior changes.
- Add Android/API-24 transport and native unreachable-server UI proof; injected failure presentation already has local controls but does not establish those runtime boundaries.
- Add broader `VpsServerStore` stress tests if future fields introduce multi-record invariants; current transaction coverage includes racing `upsert`, `remove`, and `updateStatus` operations.
- Add empty peer-list coverage for server detail before any VPN users are added.
- Retain the [read-only wizard controls](#read-only-detectplan-wizard-controls--143) for state, Back/Apply confirmation and lifecycle cancellation; add the remaining password-masking and revoked-user action controls.
- Reuse `VpsDeployFixtureData` for deterministic VPS fleet/detail/user test states: online, provisioning, attention, empty peers, active peer, idle peer, and revoked-user action disabling.

### Phase 3 — Add Visual And Smoke Coverage

- Select the screenshot framework and add deterministic fixtures with fixed data and fixed font scale.
- Add mandatory dark-mode goldens for redesigned screens because vpn0wn is dark-first.
- Add RTL goldens for custom rows, rails, headers, overlays, and bottom sheets.
- Add Espresso smoke coverage for home, detail, editor, logs, settings, add-tunnel sheet, VPS wizard navigation, QR overlay open/close, and unreachable VPS provisioning.

### Phase 4 — Add Opt-In VM Integration Tests

- `./gradlew vpsIntegrationTest` runs the disposable Ubuntu/Debian VPS integration path for install, sync, add peer, revoke peer, remove peer, backup, uninstall, reinstall, and final cleanup.
- Gate the suite behind explicit environment variables and a destructive-test guard: `TUNNL_VPS_HOST`, `TUNNL_VPS_USER`, and `TUNNL_VPS_DESTRUCTIVE=1`.
- Never run VM integration tests as part of default unit tests or default local builds.
- Repaired mutations reject unmanaged config. C9 requires a clean target, checks postconditions and limits failure cleanup to the owned upload; authorized real-server evidence remains separate.
- Use key-only SSH. Do not put passwords or private keys in the repo; optional local settings belong in ignored `.tunnl-vps-test.env`.

## Managed server snapshot controls — #196

The [snapshot contract](backend-contracts.md#managed-server-snapshot--196) adds offline coverage in `scripts/tests/provisioning_safety_test.sh`: exact UTF-8/trailing newline/CR/control preservation; absent/empty and multiple same-name historic revoked records; owned-state checks; symlink/nonregular/nested/hidden/unsupported entries, including a newline-bearing filename; NUL/invalid UTF-8; file/count/encoded-response limits; and a late read failure producing no partial secret-bearing result. Injected enumeration failures with zero or partial NUL-delimited entries must fail rather than fabricate empty/truncated history, without exposing diagnostics or bodies. A controlled held-lock admission changes config and revocations before release, then verifies a single matching generation. Independent nonblocking lock probes at both config and revoked reads prove the backup retains writer exclusion. These tests use the existing isolated mapped-root fixture and injected tools, never host root or networking.

`ProvisioningJsonTest` consumes the actual normalized script response and covers version/date/interface/marker checks, required nested types, count agreement, duplicate/unsafe archive names, distinct historical archives, byte bounds, malformed text, trailing content and redacted result diagnostics. `VpsProvisioningRequestTest` preserves no-argument backup command construction and verifies the returned snapshot through the existing client. C9's injected tests reject wrong version/date/revoked-body observations; actual destructive execution still requires its separate authorized target. Unit/source proof does not establish encrypted export, restore/rollback, destination cutover, restored handshakes/traffic, Android UI/authentication or #196 completion.

Failure controls require write/apply/postcondition rollback to the original absent/empty history, config/runtime/service state and observed live forwarding value; unknown initial forwarding or failed restoration must remain visible. Check retained private recovery evidence after incomplete rollback and committed `cleanupPath` after cleanup failure. Rerun existing install/uninstall/peer/snapshot controls because `common.sh` is shared; the new history/live-forwarding behavior remains restore-specific.

Run `VpsRestoreClientTest`, `VpsProvisioningRequestTest`, `ProvisioningJsonTest` and `VpsBackupArchiveTest` through `:ui:testDebugUnitTest` plus `:ui:detekt`. Client controls require pre-transport snapshot/request rejection, a copied history list, private mode-0600 uploads before admission, required guarded arguments, upload failure/cancellation without restore dispatch, a bounded noncancellable same-client cleanup attempt before final close, typed bounded receipts and exact request/snapshot correlation including CIDR and a different positive active peer count. Preserve original Throwable identity/suppression and reject structured destination conflicts without retry. The fake still-open client proves that removal is attempted; it does not establish deletion after concrete JSch cancellation has closed transport. The shared serializer must preserve independent archive interoperability, Unicode/bounds and unchanged envelope/KDF/password rules. Test-source inventory and the historical passes above do not replace the final revised-source gate.

These checks establish only their actual recorded source/offline scope. Real destination SSH trust/authentication, native transport, destructive restoration, restored handshakes/traffic, source/destination cutover, file-provider and app authentication/UI, physical/OEM performance, writer-format freeze and release remain separate #196 gates. The existing disposable C9 harness does not execute restore; any live restore needs its own explicitly authorized disposable destination and postconditions.

## Disposable VPS Integration Use Case

Use `vpsIntegrationTest` when provisioning scripts or app-side provisioning command contracts change and unit tests are not enough to prove the scripts still work on a real server.

This harness is for an explicitly authorized clean disposable Ubuntu/Debian VPS only. It uses system OpenSSH/scp and requires an actual root session (`id -u`), not Android transport or sudo. Existing `wg0.conf` or `/etc/wireguard/tunnl` metadata is rejected. It exclusively creates a fresh upload directory, uploads scripts, forces installation, creates/revokes/removes peers, receives a secret-bearing backup response, uninstalls, repeats installation/uninstall and checks cleanup. No cleanup is admitted before root and upload-directory ownership are confirmed. After admission, failure cleanup removes only that upload and retains server config, backup/revoke/recovery artifacts. Final artifact deletion follows both successful verified uninstalls and the initially absent metadata check. Do not run it on production or user-managed WireGuard hosts.

C9 checks JSON object/Boolean success, supported clean-target detection/planning, running `wg0`, exact saved/live peer membership after every mutation, backup shape/revoked count and both cycles' uninstall postconditions. Uninstall independently checks config/symlink and native-interface absence, requested named-package purge or kept tools, then final upload/Tunnl-directory absence. Unknown observations, malformed/extra/missing peers, partial package residue and cleanup failure return failure. Logs contain fixed local progress/category/exit text; remote fields, command arguments, streams, JSON and exception payloads are omitted. These checks observe interface/config/peer/package state, not traffic, handshake or Android integration.

Run the standard-library injected offline controls without VPS variables or network/root operations:

```sh
python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests -p test_vps_integration_test.py -v
```

The suite covers valid purge/keep-packages flows, false/unknown/malformed postconditions, exact saved/live membership, direct removal/package checks, key-only options, guard/root/upload ownership, payload-free command/JSON/nested-output failures and primary/cleanup failure preservation. See [executed proof](development.md#c9-offline-harness-postconditions--2026-10-09). This separate offline command is not wired into the default Android or destructive integration tasks. Real OpenSSH/systemd/package/packet proof still requires its own authorized disposable target.

Required environment:

```sh
TUNNL_VPS_HOST=203.0.113.10
TUNNL_VPS_USER=root
TUNNL_VPS_DESTRUCTIVE=1
```

Optional environment:

```sh
TUNNL_VPS_SSH_KEY=/path/to/private/key
TUNNL_VPS_PORT=22
TUNNL_VPS_WG_PORT=51820
TUNNL_VPS_CIDR=10.88.0.0/24
TUNNL_VPS_DNS="1.1.1.1, 1.0.0.1"
TUNNL_VPS_PURGE=1
TUNNL_PYTHON=python3
```

Run from a shell that has key-based SSH access to the VPS:

The examples show operator commands, not authorization to execute them. `.tunnl-vps-test.env` is ignored but neither task nor harness loads it automatically; export settings explicitly. OpenSSH uses `StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new`, accepting a first-seen key rather than checking an app-pinned fingerprint.

```sh
TUNNL_VPS_HOST=203.0.113.10 \
TUNNL_VPS_USER=root \
TUNNL_VPS_DESTRUCTIVE=1 \
./gradlew vpsIntegrationTest
```

On Windows, if the key is available to Windows OpenSSH but not WSL, run the Python harness directly from PowerShell:

```powershell
$env:TUNNL_VPS_HOST = '203.0.113.10'
$env:TUNNL_VPS_USER = 'root'
$env:TUNNL_VPS_DESTRUCTIVE = '1'
python scripts/vps_integration_test.py
```

With default `TUNNL_VPS_PURGE=1`, successful completion requires `wireGuardInstalled=false`, `configExists=false`, absent `wg0`, `/etc/wireguard/tunnl` and operation-owned `/root/vpn0wn-integration-<timestamp>` paths, and absence/not-installed status for the three purged packages. `TUNNL_VPS_PURGE=0` requires `packagesPurged=false` and retained `wg` availability while still checking config/interface/artifact removal. Final success prints `cleanup: uploaded scripts and vpn0wn test artifacts removed` only after absence checks pass. Cleanup failure is reported separately and keeps the primary failure; inspect retained state before another authorized run. Existing `TUNNL_*` environment variables and persisted provisioning paths are unchanged.

Security rules:

- Use key-only SSH; the harness disables password authentication.
- Never commit server passwords, private keys, generated peer configs, or local env files.
- Rotate temporary root credentials after any manual password-based setup.
- Keep `.tunnl-vps-test.env` local and ignored if you use it to store non-secret test host settings.

## Native warm-up source and physical acceptance

The shared #68 repair merged through PR #69 at `6bb17579`. Native warm-up's existing overlay includes actual Android descriptor guards; host-only aliases and virtual tests exercise both single-family cases, denied protection, unopened and closed sockets through `androidbind.ProtectSockets`. These controls do not use fake descriptor methods. At the warm-up checkpoint, only their host `conn` command disabled Go 1.24.3's module index to include virtual test files. [#73's later draft](#native-handshake-observation-controls--73-draft) also used that setting for internal `device` handshake fixtures. Before #215, host overlay commands and the production `libwg-go.so` target both appended `goindex=0` to any existing `GODEBUG`: Go 1.24.3 otherwise missed overlay-added imports, and host tests also needed overlay-added test-file discovery. This disabled module indexing for those commands while preserving compiled-package caching. The supported Go/staged-source recipe removes that override; [development setup](development.md#setup-and-source-boundaries) owns current build details. Reused `GoBackendStatisticsTest` adds the shared DOWN, service cleanup and delayed-result regressions alongside the native lifecycle suite.

The host race suite covers protected readiness at creation/rebind before concurrent cookie sends, both/one/no available families, failed protection cleanup, explicit/absent/zero port intent, requested-port retry recovery, mark-failure closure, one best-effort one-byte zero attempt per eligible initiation, expected I/O versus cancellation/closure/defect handling, destruction between sends, close/replacement generation isolation, terminal shutdown and concurrent teardown. Warm-up is enabled only by explicit nonzero ListenPort and leaves the WireGuard protocol unchanged. An ordinary `StdNetBind` peer exchanges authenticated traffic in both directions over real localhost UDP after warm-up; both peers use the globally overlaid device package. This does not establish separately compiled unmodified-peer interoperability. The actual handle owner covers a partial UAPI request blocking a query during teardown, draining admitted reads, duplicate close, late lookup and non-reused IDs. Android-side shadows verify disconnect/service cleanup can cancel a blocked statistics query and that replacement invalidates delayed results. Overlay tests reject missing/duplicate pinned-source matches and verify inspected cached upstream source files are unchanged; module download may populate an empty cache. JNI host tests attach/detach worker callbacks, clear startup-thread ownership at the boundary, preserve protection denial/destruction, retain unexpected Error identity and preserve interruption.

Host/JVM/native compilation do not establish Android VPN protection, socket accounting, wake timing or a kernel server. On 2026-10-08 (Europe/Paris), the maintainer authorized adoption through PR #63. PR #63 records exact-head ultra review/checks and actual delivery state. Separately authorized phone acceptance must cover startup, stop/restart, service destruction, network change/rebind, deep-sleep/resume and unchanged-server traffic while preserving profiles/APKs. Freeze repeated unchanged-build cellular control, fixed profile/path, success/failure observations and rejection criteria before DPI trials. Preserve the retained baseline for recovery and keep inconclusive benefit unclaimed. ### Read-only encrypted-backup comparison controls — #196
